\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{l+1}-1\end{subarray}}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},z)\}=[(p+t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}-2p^{2}+\{(p-t_{l+1}+t_{l})^{+}\}^{2}]\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle+[p^{2}-\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}-\{(p-t_{l+1}+t_{l})^{+}\}^{2}]\beta_{2}(z)+\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\beta_{3}(z).

(3) Finally, we consider
t_{L}\leq\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq T
and
t_{L}\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq T
.

(3.1) When
t_{L}-t_{L-1}\geq p
, for
t_{L}\leq\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L}+p-1
and
t_{L}\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L}+p-1
, we have
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=2
; for
t_{L}\leq\min(T,T^{\prime})\leq T
and
t_{L}\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq T
, we have
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=1
; otherwise,
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=0
. In these cases, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{L}\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq T}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},q)\}=\{(T+1-t_{L})^{2}-p^{2}\}\alpha_{1}(q)+p^{2}\alpha_{2}(q),

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{L}\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq T}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},z)\}=\{(T+1-t_{L})^{2}-p^{2}\}\beta_{1}(z)+p^{2}\beta_{2}(z).

(3.2) When
t_{L}-t_{L-1}<p
, for
t_{L}\leq\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L-1}+p-1
and
t_{L}\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L-1}+p-1
, we have
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=3
; for
t_{L}\leq\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L}+p-1
and
t_{L-1}+p\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq t_{L}+p-1
, we have
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=2
; for
t_{L}\leq\min(t,t^{\prime})\leq T
and
t_{L}+p\leq\max(t,t^{\prime})\leq T
, we have
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=1
; otherwise,
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=0
. In these cases, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{L}\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq T}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},q)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(T+1-t_{L})^{2}-p^{2}\}\alpha_{1}(q)+\{p^{2}-(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{2}\}\alpha_{2}(q)+(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{2}\alpha_{3}(q).

Combing (3.1)–(3.2), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{L}\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq T}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},q)\}=\{(T+1-t_{L})^{2}-p^{2}\}\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle+[p^{2}-\{(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{+}\}^{2}]\alpha_{2}(q)+\{(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{+}\}^{2}\alpha_{3}(q),

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{L}\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq T}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T},z)\}=\{(T+1-t_{L})^{2}-p^{2}\}\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle+[p^{2}-\{(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{+}\}^{2}]\beta_{2}(z)+\{(p-t_{L}+t_{L-1})^{+}\}^{2}\beta_{3}(z).

Therefore,

\displaystyle(T-p)^{2}\mathrm{risk}^{d}(q)=\left\{\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle+Lp^{2}\{\alpha_{2}(q)-2\alpha_{1}(q)\}+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\left[\{(p-t_{l+1}+t_{l})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right)\{\alpha_{3}(q)-2\alpha_{2}(q)+\alpha_{1}(q)\},

\displaystyle(T-p)^{2}\mathrm{risk}^{s}(z)=\left\{\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle+Lp^{2}\{\beta_{2}(z)-2\beta_{1}(z)\}+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\left[\{(p-t_{l+1}+t_{l})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right)\{\beta_{3}(z)-2\beta_{2}(z)+\beta_{1}(z)\}.

Note that, when
N\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\{4N+2q^{-1}+2\bar{q}^{-1}-8\}>0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(q)-2\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\left\{4N+4q^{-2}+4\bar{q}^{-2}-4q^{-1}-4\bar{q}^{-1}\right\}>0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}(q)-2\alpha_{2}(q)+\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\left\{2q^{-1}\left(2q^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2\bar{q}^{-1}\left(2\bar{q}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+8N\right\}\geq 0,

and when
N=1
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\{2q^{-1}+2\bar{q}^{-1}\}>0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(q)-2\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\left\{4q^{-2}+4\bar{q}^{-2}-4q^{-1}-4\bar{q}^{-1}\right\}>0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}(q)-2\alpha_{2}(q)+\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\left\{2q^{-1}\left(2q^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2\bar{q}^{-1}\left(2\bar{q}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}\right\}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\{4N+2q_{1,z}^{-1}+2q_{2,z}^{-1}-4\}>0,

\displaystyle\beta_{2}(z)-2\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{4B^{2}}{N}\left\{q_{1,z}^{-1}(q_{1,z}^{-1}-1)+q_{2,z}^{-1}(q_{2,z}^{-1}-1)\right\}>0,

\displaystyle\beta_{3}(z)-2\beta_{2}(z)+\beta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}}{N}\left\{2q_{1,z}^{-1}\left(2q_{1,z}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2q_{2,z}^{-1}\left(2q_{2,z}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+4(N-1)\right\}\geq 0.

∎

Now, we can prove
Theorem

2
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

When
\mathbb{T}\in\mathcal{T}
and
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
,
\max_{\mathbb{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{L}(1,0)
is given by

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\{(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\gamma_{1}^{d}+Lp^{2}\gamma_{2}^{d}+\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\gamma_{3}^{d}\right)B^{2},

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{1}^{d}&=\alpha_{1}(q_{1})+\alpha_{1}(q_{2})=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\{8N+2q_{1}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+2q_{2}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-16\},\\
\gamma_{2}^{d}&=\{\alpha_{2}(q_{1})-2\alpha_{1}(q_{1})\}+\{\alpha_{2}(q_{2})-2\alpha_{1}(q_{2})\}\\
&=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\left\{8N+4q_{1}^{-2}+4\bar{q}_{1}^{-2}-4q_{1}^{-1}-4\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+4q_{2}^{-2}+4\bar{q}_{2}^{-2}-4q_{2}^{-1}-4\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}\right\},\\
\gamma_{3}^{d}&=\{\alpha_{3}(q_{1})-2\alpha_{2}(q_{1})+\alpha_{1}(q_{1})\}+\{\alpha_{3}(q_{2})-2\alpha_{2}(q_{2})+\alpha_{1}(q_{2})\}=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\left\{2q_{1}^{-1}(2q_{1}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}(2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2q_{2}^{-1}(2q_{2}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}(2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-1)^{2}+16N\right\},\end{aligned}

(S8)

when
N\geq 2
, and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{1}^{d}&=\alpha_{1}(q_{1})+\alpha_{1}(q_{2})=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\{2q_{1}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+2q_{2}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}\},\\
\gamma_{2}^{d}&=\{\alpha_{2}(q_{1})-2\alpha_{1}(q_{1})\}+\{\alpha_{2}(q_{2})-2\alpha_{1}(q_{2})\}\\
&=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\left\{4q_{1}^{-2}+4\bar{q}_{1}^{-2}-4q_{1}^{-1}-4\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+4q_{2}^{-2}+4\bar{q}_{2}^{-2}-4q_{2}^{-1}-4\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}\right\},\\
\gamma_{3}^{d}&=\{\alpha_{3}(q_{1})-2\alpha_{2}(q_{1})+\alpha_{1}(q_{1})\}+\{\alpha_{3}(q_{2})-2\alpha_{2}(q_{2})+\alpha_{1}(q_{2})\}=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\left\{2q_{1}^{-1}(2q_{1}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}(2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2q_{2}^{-1}(2q_{2}^{-1}-1)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}(2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-1)^{2}\right\},\end{aligned}

(S9)

when
N=1
.

Similarly, when
\mathbb{T}\in\mathcal{T}
and
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
,
\max_{\mathbb{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{L}(0,1)
is given by

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\{(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\gamma_{1}^{s}+Lp^{2}\gamma_{2}^{s}+\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\gamma_{3}^{s}\right)B^{2},

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{1}^{s}&=\beta_{1}(1)+\beta_{1}(0)=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\{8N+2q_{1}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+2q_{2}^{-1}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-8\},\\
\gamma_{2}^{s}&=\{\beta_{2}(1)-2\beta_{1}(1)\}+\{\beta_{2}(0)-2\beta_{1}(0)\}\\
&=\frac{4}{N(T-p)^{2}}\left\{q_{1}^{-1}(q_{1}^{-1}-1)+\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}(\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}-1)+q_{2}^{-1}(q_{2}^{-1}-1)+\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}(\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-1)\right\},\\
\gamma_{3}^{s}&=\{\beta_{3}(1)-2\beta_{2}(1)+\beta_{1}(1)\}+\{\beta_{3}(0)-2\beta_{2}(0)+\beta_{1}(0)\}=\frac{1}{N(T-p)^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\Big\{2q_{1}^{-1}\left(2q_{1}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}\left(2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2q_{2}^{-1}\left(2q_{2}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}\left(2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}-1\right)^{2}\\
&\qquad+4(N-1)\Big\}.\end{aligned}

(S10)

The weighted sum
\max_{\mathbb{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{d},\psi_{s})
, is given by

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\{(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\gamma_{1}^{*}+Lp^{2}\gamma_{2}^{*}+\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\gamma_{3}^{*}\right)B^{2},

with
\gamma_{J}^{*}=\psi_{d}\gamma_{J}^{d}+\psi_{s}\gamma_{J}^{s}
for
J=1,2,3
. Based on
B^{2}>0
, obtaining the minimax optimal design is then equivalent to minimizing

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbb{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{d},\psi_{s})/B^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\{(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\gamma_{1}^{*}+Lp^{2}\gamma_{2}^{*}+\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\gamma_{3}^{*}.

∎

To simplify the notation, we define

\displaystyle\theta^{*}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{*}}{\gamma_{1}^{*}}=\frac{4N\psi_{d}I(N\geq 2)+2q_{1}^{-2}+2\bar{q}_{1}^{-2}-2q_{1}^{-1}-2\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+2q_{2}^{-2}+2\bar{q}_{2}^{-2}-2q_{2}^{-1}-2\bar{q}_{2}^{-1}}{(4N-4-4\psi_{d})I(N\geq 2)+(q_{1}^{-1}+\bar{q}_{1}^{-1}+q_{2}^{-1}+\bar{q}_{2}^{-1})},

(S11)

where
I(\cdot)
is the indicator function. Then
\max_{\mathbb{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{d},\psi_{s})/B^{2}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1+\theta^{*}L)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})\right\}\gamma_{1}^{*}B^{2}+\left(\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right)\gamma_{3}^{*}B^{2}.

(S12)

E
A polynomial–time algorithm to solve the integer optimization problem

In this section, we propose a polynomial–time algorithm to solve the integer optimization problem stated in
Theorem

2
.

Lemma S6
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
and with
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
, any design minimizing

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}+(L-1+\theta^{*}L)p^{2}+2p(t_{L}-t_{1})

(S13)

must satisfy

\displaystyle|(t_{1}-t_{0})-(t_{L+1}-t_{L})|\leq 1,\quad|(t_{l+1}-t_{l})-(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})|\leq 1,\quad\forall 1\leq l,l^{\prime}\leq L-1.

Proof of
Lemma

S6
.

We will prove the lemma by contradiction.

(1) Assume there exists a design
\mathbb{T}
such that
|(t_{1}-t_{0})-(t_{L+1}-t_{L})|\geq 2
. We will construct a new design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
with a lower value of
Equation

S13
. Without loss of generality, let us assume
(t_{1}-t_{0})-(t_{L+1}-t_{L})\geq 2
. The case where
(t_{L+1}-t_{L})-(t_{1}-t_{0})\geq 2
can be addressed similarly. Consider the design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}=\{\tilde{t}_{0}=1,\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{1}-1,\tilde{t}_{2}=t_{2}-1,\ldots,\tilde{t}_{L-1}=t_{L-1}-1,\tilde{t}_{L}=t_{L}-1\}
. Notably, we have
\tilde{t}_{l+1}-\tilde{t}_{l}=t_{l+1}-t_{l}
for
l=1,\ldots,L-1
. The change in the first term of
Equation

S13
is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}(\tilde{t}_{l+1}-\tilde{t}_{l})^{2}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(\tilde{t}_{L+1}-\tilde{t}_{L})^{2}+(\tilde{t}_{1}-\tilde{t}_{0})^{2}\}-\{(t_{L+1}-t_{L})^{2}+(t_{1}-t_{0})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(t_{L+1}-t_{L}+1)^{2}+(t_{1}-t_{0}-1)^{2}\}-\{(t_{L+1}-t_{L})^{2}+(t_{1}-t_{0})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\{(t_{1}-t_{0})-(t_{L+1}-t_{L})-1\}<0.

The second and third terms remain unchanged because
L
remains unchanged and
\tilde{t}_{L}-\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{L}-t_{1}
. Thus, there exists a design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
with a lower value of
Equation

S13
than
\mathbb{T}
when
(t_{1}-t_{0})-(t_{L+1}-t_{L})\geq 2
. The case where
(t_{L+1}-t_{L})-(t_{1}-t_{0})\geq 2
can be handled similarly by defining
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}=\{\tilde{t}_{0}=1,\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{1}+1,\tilde{t}_{2}=t_{2}+1,\ldots,\tilde{t}_{L-1}=t_{L-1}+1,\tilde{t}_{L}=t_{L}+1\}
.

(2) Assume there exists a design
\mathbb{T}
such that
|(t_{l+1}-t_{l})-(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})|\geq 2
for some
1\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq L+1
. We will construct a new design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
with a lower value of
Equation

S13
. Without loss of generality, let us assume that
(t_{l+1}-t_{l})-(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})\geq 2
. The case where
(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})-(t_{l+1}-t_{l})\geq 2
can be addressed similarly. Consider the design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}=\{\tilde{t}_{0}=1,\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{1},\ldots,\tilde{t}_{l}=t_{l},\tilde{t}_{l+1}=t_{l+1}-1,\ldots,\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}}=t_{l^{\prime}}-1,\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}+1}=t_{l^{\prime}+1},\ldots,\tilde{t}_{L}=t_{L}\}
. Notably, we have
\tilde{t}_{\ell+1}-\tilde{t}_{\ell}=t_{\ell+1}-t_{\ell}
when
\ell\neq l,l^{\prime}
. The change in the first term in
Equation

S13
is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}(\tilde{t}_{l+1}-\tilde{t}_{l})^{2}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}+1}-\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}})^{2}+(\tilde{t}_{l+1}-\tilde{t}_{l})^{2}\}-\{(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})^{2}+(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}}+1)^{2}+(t_{l+1}-t_{l}-1)^{2}\}-\{(t_{L+1}-t_{L})^{2}+(t_{1}-t_{0})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\{(t_{l+1}-t_{l})-(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})-1\}<0.

The second and third terms remain unchanged because
L
remains unchanged and
\tilde{t}_{L}-\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{L}-t_{1}
. Thus, there exists a design
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
with a lower value of
Equation

S13
than
\mathbb{T}
when
(t_{l+1}-t_{l})-(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})\geq 2
. The case where
(t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}})-(t_{l+1}-t_{l})\geq 2
can be handled similarly by defining
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}=\{\tilde{t}_{0}=1,\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{1},\ldots,\tilde{t}_{l}=t_{l},\tilde{t}_{l+1}=t_{l+1}+1,\ldots,\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}}=t_{l^{\prime}}+1,\tilde{t}_{l^{\prime}+1}=t_{l^{\prime}+1},\ldots,\tilde{t}_{L}=t_{L}\}
.
∎

Based on
Lemma

S6
, a design that minimizes
Equation

S13
can be characterized by two integers:
a=\min\{t_{1}-t_{0},t_{L+1}-t_{L}\}
and
b=\min_{l=1,\ldots,L-1}\{t_{l+1}-t_{l}\}
. Another important value in the objective function is
L
.
Although the integer
L
is unknown, it can be computed using
T
,
a
,
b
and
\theta^{*}
under two cases, as discussed in
Lemma

S7
.

Lemma S7
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
and
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
, given a candidate integer pair
(a,b)
to characterize a design
\mathbb{T}
, we consider two cases:
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
(Case 1) or
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
(Case 2).

(1.1) For the case where
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
and
\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, we have
L=\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor+1
;

(1.2) for the case where
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
and
\theta^{*}>b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, we have
L=\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil+1
;

(2.1) for the case where
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
and
\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, we have
L=\lfloor(T-2a-1)/b\rfloor+1
;

(2.2) for the case where
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
and
\theta^{*}>b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, we have
L=\lceil(T-2a-1)/(b+1)\rceil+1
.

Proof of
Lemma

S7
.

To determine the value of
L
, it is crucial to explicitly assess the number of time periods of lengths
b
and
b+1
. Our analysis primarily focuses on comparing the objective function related to
b
time periods of length
b+1
with the objective function associated with
b+1
time periods of length
b
. The difference between the objective functions, as presented in
Equation

S13
, is given by

b(b+1)-(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}
.

When this difference is greater than 0, it suggests that we should prioritize maximizing the use of time periods of length
b
. Conversely, when the difference is less than 0, it indicates that we should maximize the utilization of time periods of length
b+1
.

When
b(b+1)-(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\geq 0
, i.e.,
\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, it indicates that we should maximize the use of time periods of length
b
. Under this circumstance, we differentiate between two cases:

(1.1) In Case 1,
L=\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor+1
, and the the time period between
t_{1}
and
t_{L}
will be divided into
\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor(b+1)-(T-2a)
time periods of length
b
and
(T-2a)-\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor b
time periods of length
b+1
. The objective function in
Equation

S13
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &2a^{2}+\left\{\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor(b+1)-(T-2a)\right\}b^{2}\\
&+\left\{(T-2a)-\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor b\right\}(b+1)^{2}\\
&+\left\{\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor+\theta^{*}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor+1\right)\right\}p^{2}+2p(T-2a)\\
=&2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)(2b+2p+1)+\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor\{(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}-b(b+1)\}.\end{aligned}

(S14)

This equation implies that
\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor\in[(T-2a)/(b+1),(T-2a)/b]
, for the coefficients of both
b^{2}
and
(b+1)^{2}
must be non-negative.

(2.1) In Case 2,
L=\lfloor(T-2a-1)/b\rfloor+1
, and the time period between
t_{1}
and
t_{L}
will be divided into
\lfloor(T-2a-1)/b\rfloor(b+1)-(T-2a-1)
time periods of length
b
and
(T-2a-1)-\lfloor(T-2a-1)/b\rfloor b
time periods of length
b+1
. The objective function
Equation

S13
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &a^{2}+(a+1)^{2}+\left\{\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a-1}{b}\right\rfloor(b+1)-(T-2a-1)\right\}b^{2}\\
&+\left\{(T-2a-1)-\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a-1}{b}\right\rfloor b\right\}(b+1)^{2}\\
&+\left\{\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a-1}{b}\right\rfloor+\theta^{*}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a-1}{b}\right\rfloor+1\right)\right\}p^{2}+2p(T-2a-1)\\
=&a^{2}+(a+1)^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a-1)(2b+2p+1)\\
&+\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a-1}{b}\right\rfloor\{(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}-b(b+1)\}.\end{aligned}

(S15)

The equation implies that
\lfloor(T-2a-1)/b\rfloor\in[(T-2a-1)/(b+1),(T-2a-1)/b]
, for the coefficients of both
b^{2}
and
(b+1)^{2}
must be non-negative.

When
b(b+1)-(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}<0
, i.e.,
\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
, it indicates that we should maximize the use of time periods of length
b+1
. Under this circumstance, we differentiate between two cases:

(1.2) In Case 1,
L=\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil+1
, and the the time period between
t_{1}
and
t_{L}
will be divided into
\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil(b+1)-(T-2a)
time periods of length
b
and
(T-2a)-\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil b
time periods of length
b+1
. The objective function in
Equation

S13
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &2a^{2}+\left\{\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil(b+1)-(T-2a)\right\}b^{2}\\
&+\left\{(T-2a)-\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil b\right\}(b+1)^{2}\\
&+\left\{\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil+\theta^{*}\left(\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil+1\right)\right\}p^{2}+2p(T-2a)\\
=&2a^{2}+(T-2a)(2b+2p+1)+\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil\{(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}-b(b+1)\}.\end{aligned}

(S16)

This equation implies that
\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil\in[(T-2a)/(b+1),(T-2a)/b]
, for the coefficients of both
b^{2}
and
(b+1)^{2}
must be non-negative.

(2.2) In Case 2,
L=\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil+1
, and the time period between
t_{1}
and
t_{L}
will be divided into
\lceil(T-2a-1)/(b+1)\rceil(b+1)-(T-2a-1)
time periods of length
b
and
(T-2a-1)-\lceil(T-2a-1)/(b+1)\rceil b
time periods of length
b+1
. The objective function in
Equation

S13
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &a^{2}+(a+1)^{2}+\left\{\left\lceil\frac{T-2a-1}{b+1}\right\rceil(b+1)-(T-2a-1)\right\}b^{2}\\
&+\left\{(T-2a-1)-\left\lceil\frac{T-2a-1}{b+1}\right\rceil b\right\}(b+1)^{2}\\
&+\left\{\left\lceil\frac{T-2a-1}{b+1}\right\rceil+\theta^{*}\left(\left\lceil\frac{T-2a-1}{b+1}\right\rceil+1\right)\right\}p^{2}+2p(T-2a-1)\\
=&a^{2}+(a+1)^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a-1)(2b+2p+1)\\
&+\left\lceil\frac{T-2a-1}{b+1}\right\rceil\{(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}-b(b+1)\}.\end{aligned}

(S17)

This equation implies that
\lceil(T-2a-1)/(b+1)\rceil\in[(T-2a-1)/(b+1),(T-2a-1)/b]
, for the coefficients of both
b^{2}
and
(b+1)^{2}
must be non-negative.
∎

Without additional information, the number of candidate pairs
(a,b)
is
O(T^{2})
, since both
a
and
b
are integers less than
T
. Furthermore, we have the constraints
a\geq p+1
as specified in
Lemma

S3
, and
b\geq p/2
according to
Lemma

S4
. For each candidate pair
(a,b)
, we need to evaluate the expression in
Equation

S13
under two scenarios: when
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
and when
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
. By comparing the results from all candidate pairs across these two cases, we can determine the design that minimizes the value of
Equation

S13
.

It is important to note that whether
t_{l+1}-t_{l}=b
or
t_{l+1}-t_{l}=b+1
does not affect the value of
Equation

S13
. Instead, the value depends solely on the counts of indices
l
for which
t_{l+1}-t_{l}=b
and
l^{\prime}
for which
t_{l^{\prime}+1}-t_{l^{\prime}}=b+1
. Consequently, if
(t_{L}-t_{1})
is not a multiple of
b
, we can identify a class of designs with different arrangements of
b
and
b+1
that yield the same value of
Equation

S13
. Additionally, in the case where
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
, we can choose
t_{1}-t_{0}=a
and
t_{L+1}-t_{L}=a+1
or vice versa, resulting in two designs that produce the same value for
Equation

S13
.

From the discussions outlined, we conclude that the time complexity of the proposed polynomial algorithm is
O(T^{2})
, for we only need to iterate through all possible integer pairs
(a,b)
to identify the design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
with
(a^{*},b^{*})
that minimizes
Equation

S13
.

However, apart from the first term, which is associated with
Equation

S13
, there is another term in the objective function in
Equation

S12
:

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right)\gamma_{3}^{*}B^{2}.

Its value is not less than 0 and reaches its minimum 0 when
b\geq p
. We provide an additional lemma to show the design that minimizes
Equation

S13
is precisely the minimax optimal design.

Lemma S8
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
and
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
, if there exists a design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
that minimizes
Equation

S13
, it is precisely the minimax optimal design as described in
Theorem

2
.

Proof of
Lemma

S8
.

The minimax optimal design is just the design that minimizes
Equation

S12
. This equation consists of two terms: the first term is linked to
Equation

S13
and is minimized when
Equation

S13
is minimized, while the second term is given by:

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right)\gamma_{3}^{*}B^{2}.

Since
\gamma_{3}^{*}>0
, to demonstrate that the design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
minimizing
Equation

S13
is indeed the minimax optimal design, we must show that it also minimizes

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=2}^{L}\left[\{(p-t_{l}+t_{l-1})^{+}\}^{2}\right]\right).

(S18)

Notably, the value of
Equation

S18
reaches its minimum 0 when
b\geq p
. We will prove that the lower bound of
Equation

S13
when
b\in[p/2,p-1]
is greater than the upper bound of that when
b=p
. Therefore, we can conclude that the value of
b^{*}
in
\mathbb{T}^{*}
must be greater than
p
, which implies that it will also minimize
Equation

S18
.
In Case 1, when
b\in[p/2,p-1]
, we have
-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}>0
, which is the Case (1.2) stated in
Lemma

S7
, and
Equation

S16
is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+2b+1\}+\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil\{-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\}.

Based on the property
\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil\in[(T-2a)/(b+1),(T-2a)/b]
as stated in the proof of
Lemma

S7
, the lower bound of
Equation

S16
can be established as:

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/(b+1)\}.

(S19)

When
b=p
and
-p(p+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}>0
, which is the Case (1.2) stated in
Lemma

S7
, the upper bound of
Equation

S16
is

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+p+(\theta^{*}+1)p\},

and when
b=p
and
-p(p+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\leq 0
, which is the Case (1.1) stated in
Lemma

S7
, the upper bound of
Equation

S16
is

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+p+(\theta^{*}+1)p\}.

Both values are lower than or equal to
Equation

S19
, implying that
b^{*}\geq p
.

The proof for Case 2 follows a similar structure to that of Case 1.
∎

Based on Lemmas
S6
–
S8
, we can give a polynomial–time algorithm with a time complexity of
O(T^{2})
to detect the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
. The algorithm is outlined as follows:

Algorithm S1

Minimax optimal design detection

1:

Input:

T
,
N
,
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
,
p

2:

Calculate
\theta^{*}
as shown in
Equation

S11
.

3:

for
each candidate pair
(a,b)
such that
a\geq p+1
and
b\geq p/2

do

4:

Calculate the objective function in Case 1:

5:

if

\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1

then

6:

Use
Equation

S14

7:

else

8:

Use
Equation

S16

9:

end

if

10:

Calculate the objective function in Case 2:

11:

if

\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1

then

12:

Use
Equation

S15

13:

else

14:

Use
Equation

S17

15:

end

if

16:

end

for

17:

Identify the pair and case that yields the lowest value of the objective function.

18:

Output:
A class of minimax optimal designs characterized by
(a^{*},b^{*})
and Case 1 or 2.

F
Proof of Corollary
1

Lemma S9

(Minimax optimal design with specific
T
and
\theta^{*}
)
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
and
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
, we have
(1) when
p=0
, the minimax optimal design is given by
\{1,2,3,\ldots,T\}
;
and (2) when
p>0
, we have:

(2.1) for an integer pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
with
b^{*}\in[\{-1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2,\{1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2]
and
a^{*}\in[\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}-1\}/2,\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}+1\}/2]
, if
T-2a^{*}
is a multiple of
b^{*}
with
(T-2a^{*})/b^{*}=K-4\geq 0
, then the minimax optimal design is given by
\{1,a^{*}+1,a^{*}+b^{*}+1,a^{*}+2b^{*}+1,\ldots,a^{*}+(K-4)b^{*}+1\}
;

(2.2) for an integer pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
with
b^{*}\in[\{-1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2,\{1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2]
and
a^{*}\in[\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}/2,\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}+2\}/2]
, if
T-2a^{*}-1
is a multiple of
b^{*}
with
(T-2a^{*}-1)/b^{*}=K-4\geq 0
, then the minimax optimal design is given by

\{1,a^{*}+1,a^{*}+b^{*}+1,a^{*}+2b^{*}+1,\ldots,a^{*}+(K-4)b^{*}+1\}
or
\{1,a^{*}+2,a^{*}+b^{*}+2,a^{*}+2b^{*}+2,\ldots,a^{*}+(K-4)b^{*}+2\}
.

Proof of
Lemma

S9
.

By
Lemma

S3
and
Lemma

S4
, we need to minimize the objective function under the constraints
t_{1}\geq p+2
,
t_{L}\leq T-p
, and
t_{l+1}-t_{l-1}\geq p
for
l=1,\ldots,L-1
.

(1) When
p=0
, we notice that the coefficient of
\gamma_{1}^{*}
is
\sum_{l=0}^{L}(t_{l+1}-t_{l})^{2}
, which is minimized when
t_{l+1}-t_{l}=1
for
l=0,\ldots,L
. Furthermore, the coefficients of
\gamma_{3}^{*}
is 0. As a result, the minimax optimal design is
\{1,2,3,\ldots,T\}
.

(2.1.1) When
p\neq 0
and
\theta^{*}\leq b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
,
Equation

S14
, the coefficient of
\gamma_{1}^{*}
in Case 1 (
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
), is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+2b+1\}+\left\lfloor\frac{T-2a}{b}\right\rfloor\{-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\}.

If we want to prove that the objective function reaches the minimum under an integer pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
, an approach is to compare the objective function under this integer pair with the lower bounds of the objective function under other candidate integer pairs. Based on
\lfloor(T-2a)/b\rfloor\leq(T-2a)/b
, the lower bound of the objective function is

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+2b+1\}+\frac{T-2a}{b}\{-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b\}.

If
T-2a
is a multiple of
b
, the objective function is also
2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b\}
.
For any integer
a
, an integer
b^{*}
that minimizes
2p+b+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b
must satisfy
2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\leq 2p+(b^{*}-1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/(b^{*}-1)
and
2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\leq 2p+(b^{*}+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/(b^{*}+1)
. It implies that the optimal
b^{*}
should satisfy
b^{*}\in[\{-1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2,\{1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2]
.

Additionally, consider the remaining term associated with
a
:
2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}
. Similarly, an integer
a^{*}
that minimizes
2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}
must satisfy
2(a^{*})^{2}+(T-2a^{*})\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}\leq 2(a^{*}+1)^{2}+(T-2a^{*}-1)\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}
and
2(a^{*})^{2}+(T-2a^{*})\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}\leq 2(a^{*}-1)^{2}+(T-2a^{*}+2)\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}\}
. It implies that
a^{*}\in[\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}-1\}/2,\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}+1\}/2]
.

As a result, for an integer pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
with
b^{*}\in[\{-1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2,\{1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2]
and
a^{*}\in[\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}-1\}/2,\{2p+b^{*}+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/b^{*}+1\}/2]
, if
T-2a^{*}
is a multiple of
b^{*}
with
(T-2a^{*})/b^{*}=K-4\geq 0
, the minimax optimal design is given by
\{1,a^{*}+1,(a^{*}+1)+b^{*},\ldots,a^{*}+(K-4)b^{*}+1\}
.

(2.1.2) When
p\neq 0
and
\theta^{*}>b(b+1)/p^{2}-1
,
Equation

S16
, the objective function in Case 1 (
t_{1}-t_{0}=t_{L+1}-t_{L}
), is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+2b+1\}+\left\lceil\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\right\rceil\{-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\}.

We similarly consider its lower bound.
Based on
\lceil(T-2a)/(b+1)\rceil\geq(T-2a)/(b+1)
, the lower bound is

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+2b+1\}+\frac{T-2a}{b+1}\{-b(b+1)+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b+1+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/(b+1)\}.

If
T-2a
is a multiple of
b+1
, the objective function is also
2a^{2}+\theta^{*}p^{2}+(T-2a)\{2p+b+1+(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}/(b+1)\}
. With a similar manner to that in (2.1.1), we can prove that for any
a
, the optimal
(b^{*}+1)\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies
\{-1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2\leq b^{*}+1\leq\{1+\sqrt{1+4(\theta^{*}+1)p^{2}}\}/2
. Replacing
b^{*}+1
with
b^{*}
, we find that the result is identical to (2.1.1). Consequently, we can derive (2.1) in
Lemma

S9
by combining (2.1.1) and (2.1.2).

(2.2)
The proof for Case 2 (
t_{1}-t_{0}\neq t_{L+1}-t_{L}
) follows a similar structure to that of (2.1.1) and (2.1.2).

While sequential optimization is typically unfeasible in integer programming, this problem is unique in that the optimal
b
remains constant for different values of
a
. Suppose there exists a pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
that minimizes the objective function. If
b^{*}
were to take on any other value
b^{\prime}
, it would allow for a pair
(a^{*},b^{*})
with a larger value of the objective function. The result for
a
is similar: other values of
a
would result in a larger objective function value.

∎

Based on
Lemma

S9
, we can prove
Corollary

1
.

Proof of
Corollary

1
.

We only need to consider the two typical cases:
\theta^{*}\leq 1/p
and
\theta^{*}\in(1/p,(3p+2)/p^{2}]
.

When
\theta^{*}\leq 1/p
, we have
a^{*}=2p
and
b^{*}=p
based on
Lemma

S9
. Therefore, when
T-4p
is a multiple of
p
with
(T-4p)/p=K-4\geq 0
, the minimax optimal design is
\{1,2p+1,3p+1,\ldots,(K-2)p+1\}
.

When
\theta^{*}\leq(3p+2)/p^{2}
, we have
a^{*}=2p+1
and
b^{*}=p+1
. Therefore, when
T-4p-2
is a multiple of
p+1
with
(T-4p-2)/(p+1)=K-4\geq 0
, the minimax optimal design is
\mathbb{T}^{*}_{2}=\{1,2p+2,3p+3,\ldots,(K-2)(p+1)\}
.
∎

G
Proof of Theorem
S1

Proof of Theorem
S1
.

Under the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
with
(T-2a^{*})/b^{*}=K-4\geq 0
, define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i,1}(q\mathbf{1},z\mathbf{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{t_{2}-1}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1},z\mathbf{1}),\ \tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i,k}(q\mathbf{1},z\mathbf{1})=\sum_{t=t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}-1}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1},z\mathbf{1}),\quad\text{for}\quad k=2,\ldots,K-2,

Then,

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)-\tau^{d}(q)=\frac{1}{T-p}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q),\quad\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)\}=\frac{1}{(T-p)^{2}}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\right\},

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)-\tau^{s}(z)=\frac{1}{T-p}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z),\quad\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)\}=\frac{1}{(T-p)^{2}}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\right\}.

(1) By
Lemma

S1
, we have
E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\}=E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\}=0
. Thus,

E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}=0
and
E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}=0
.

(2) We consider the expectation of
\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}^{2}
:
when
k=1
,

\displaystyle E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{t_{2}-1}\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{t_{2}-1}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\}^{2}+2\sum_{p+1\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{2}-1}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\},

and when
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left\{\sum_{t=t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}-1}\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}-1}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\}^{2}+2\sum_{t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k+1}-1}E\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\}.

By
Lemma

S1
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\left\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2^{J_{t}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t}+1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t}}(q^{-J_{t}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\right\}^{2}+2^{J_{t}}(\bar{q}^{-J_{t}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}^{2},

and when
p+1\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{2}-1
or
t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k+1}-1
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\left\{\mathbf{D}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\mathbf{D}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}(q^{-J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}(\bar{q}^{-J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\right\}.

Considering
J_{t}=J_{t^{\prime}}=2
when
t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k}+p-1
and
J_{t}=J_{t^{\prime}}=J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=1
otherwise, and
\eta_{1}=1
and
\eta_{k}=2
otherwise under the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{2}(q)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(\bar{q}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-2\mathbf{J}_{N}+4\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle+\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{J}_{N}+(2^{\eta_{k}}q^{-\eta_{k}}-2q^{-1}-2^{\eta_{k}}+2)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{J}_{N}+(2^{\eta_{k}}\bar{q}^{-\eta_{k}}-2\bar{q}^{-1}-2^{\eta_{k}}+2)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle+\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-2(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}.

Then we know

\displaystyle N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{2}(q)\}

\displaystyle=A^{d}+B^{d}+C^{d}.

(3) Considering
\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}^{2}
, we have: when
k=1
,

\displaystyle E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{t_{2}-1}\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{t_{2}-1}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\}^{2}+2\sum_{p+1\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{2}-1}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\},

and when
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left\{\sum_{t=t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}-1}\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}-1}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\}^{2}+2\sum_{t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k+1}-1}E\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\}.

By
Lemma

S1
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\left\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2^{J_{t}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}^{2}+(2^{J_{t}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t}}(q_{1,z}^{-J_{t}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}^{2}+2^{J_{t}}(q_{2,z}^{-J_{t}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}^{2},

and when
p+1\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{2}-1
or
t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k+1}-1
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\left\{\mathbf{S}_{t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\mathbf{S}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+(2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}+\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}(q_{1,z}^{-J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+2^{J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}(q_{2,z}^{-J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{Y_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})Y_{i,t^{\prime}}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\right\}.

Considering
J_{t}=J_{t^{\prime}}=2
when
t_{k}\leq t<t^{\prime}\leq t_{k}+p-1
and
J_{t}=J_{t^{\prime}}=J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=1
otherwise, and
\eta_{1}=1
and
\eta_{k}=2
otherwise under the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q_{1,z}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q_{2,z}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{2\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{J}_{N}+(2^{\eta_{k}}q_{1,z}^{-\eta_{k}}-2q_{1,z}^{-1}-2^{\eta_{k}}+2)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{(2^{\eta_{k}}-2)\mathbf{J}_{N}+(2^{\eta_{k}}q_{2,z}^{-\eta_{k}}-2q_{2,z}^{-1}-2^{\eta_{k}}+2)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}.

Considering
\eta_{1}=1
and
\eta_{k}=2
when
k\geq 2
, we know

\displaystyle N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}^{2}(z)\}

\displaystyle=A^{s}+B^{s}+C^{s}.

(4) We consider the expectations of
\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k+1}(q)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k+1}(z)
, where
k=1,\ldots,K-3
, as other cross-terms are all 0.
Considering that
J_{t,t^{\prime}}^{\circ}=1
under the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
when
p+1\leq t\leq t_{2}-1
,
t_{2}\leq t^{\prime}\leq t_{3}-1
or

,
t_{k+1}\leq t^{\prime}\leq t_{k+2}-1
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k+1}(q)\}

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(\bar{q}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-\mathbf{J}_{N}+2\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{-\mathbf{J}_{N}+2\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\},

and

\displaystyle N^{2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k+1}(z)\}

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q_{1,z}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}+2(q_{2,z}^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}.

Then we know

\displaystyle 2N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k+1}(q)\}

\displaystyle=D^{d}+E^{d}+F^{d}+G^{d},

\displaystyle 2N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k+1}(z)\}

\displaystyle=D^{s}+E^{s}+F^{s}+G^{s}.

The variance can be calculated by

\displaystyle N^{2}(T-p)^{2}\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)\}

\displaystyle=N^{2}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\right\}

\displaystyle=N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}^{2}+2N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k+1}(q)\},

\displaystyle N^{2}(T-p)^{2}\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)\}

\displaystyle=N^{2}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\right\}

\displaystyle=N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\}^{2}+2N^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}E\{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k}(z)\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{k+1}(z)\}.

∎

H
Proof of
Corollary

S1

Proof of
Corollary

S1
.

To estimate
E\{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}(q)\}^{2}
, we notice that all terms can be unbiasedly estimated except the terms with
\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})
(in
C^{d}
),
\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})
(in
C^{d}
),
\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{i,k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})
(in
F^{d}
) and
\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\check{Y}_{i,k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
(in
G^{d}
). To solve this issue, we conservatively estimate them with
2^{-1}[\{\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{2}+\{\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{2}]
,
2^{-1}[\{\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{2}+\{\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{2}]
,
2^{-1}[\{\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{2}+\{\check{Y}_{i,k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{2}]
, and
2^{-1}[\{\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{2}+\{\check{Y}_{i,k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{2}]
, respectively, according to Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. Then an upper bound for
C^{d}
is

\displaystyle\bar{C}^{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-2(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-4(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\},

where
\mathbf{J}_{N}
denotes the
N\times N
matrix of ones and
\mathbf{I}_{N}
denotes the
N\times N
identity matrix.
Similarly, upper bounds for
F^{d}
and
G^{d}
are

\displaystyle\bar{F}^{d}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{-(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=2}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\},

\displaystyle\bar{G}^{d}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{-(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k+1}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})\}.

Similar to direct effects, we can conservatively estimate the cross terms by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. An upper bound for
C^{s}
is

\displaystyle\bar{C}^{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}.

Upper bounds for
F^{s}
and
G^{s}
are

\displaystyle\bar{F}^{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=2}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\},

\displaystyle\bar{G}^{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=2}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-3}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\{\mathbf{J}_{N}\}\{\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})\}.

∎

I
Proof of
Theorem

S2

To prove that the estimators are conservative, we first introduce a lemma established by
Delevoye and Sävje, (2020)
. Suppose that the quantity of interest is
\mu=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}y_{i}
and its Horvitz–Thompson estimator is
\hat{\mu}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nu_{i}\omega_{i}y_{i}
, where
\nu_{i}
is the indicator function taking the value 1 when the unit
i
is in the sample and 0 otherwise, and
\omega_{i}=1/\pi_{i}
with
\pi_{i}=E(\nu_{i})
being the inclusion probability that the unit
i
is in the sample. The Horvitz–Thompson estimator is unbiased because
E(\nu_{i}\omega_{i})=1
. Let
\bar{\pi}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\pi_{i}
denote the average inclusion probability,
\tilde{\pi}_{i}=\pi_{i}/\bar{\pi}
denote the normalized version of
\pi_{i}
, and
\tilde{\omega}_{i}=\bar{\pi}\omega_{i}=1/\tilde{\pi}_{i}
denote the normalized version of
\omega_{i}
.

Assumption S1

(Condition 1 of
Delevoye and Sävje, (2020)
)
.

There exist
\rho_{y}>2
and
\rho_{\omega}>1
with
\rho_{y}\rho_{\omega}\geq\rho_{y}+2\rho_{\omega}
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|y_{i}|^{\rho_{y}}\right\}^{1/\rho_{y}}\leq\kappa_{y},\quad\left\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{\omega}_{i}^{\rho_{\omega}}\right\}^{1/\rho_{\omega}}\leq\kappa_{\omega}.

Lemma S10

(Lemma 1 of
Delevoye and Sävje, (2020)
)
.

Let
\pi_{i\mid j}
be the inclusion probability of unit
i
conditional on that unit
j
is sampled and
\tilde{\pi}_{i\mid j}=\pi_{i\mid j}/\bar{\pi}
be the normalized version of
\pi_{i\mid j}
. Define

\displaystyle H(h)=\left\{\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}|\tilde{\pi}_{i\mid j}-\tilde{\pi}_{i}|^{1/h}\right\}^{h}.

Under
Assumption

S1
,
\mathrm{var}(\hat{\mu})\leq\kappa_{y}^{2}\kappa_{\omega}/\bar{\pi}N+\kappa_{y}^{2}\kappa_{\omega}H(1-1/\rho_{y}-1/\rho_{\omega})
.

Proof of
Theorem

S2
.

The conservative variance estimators
\hat{A}^{*},\hat{B}^{*},\hat{\bar{C}}^{*},\hat{D}^{*},\hat{E}^{*},\hat{\bar{F}}^{*},\hat{\bar{G}}^{*}
(
*=d,s
) presented in
Theorem

S2
are, in fact, the Horvitz–Thompson estimators of the upper bounds provided in
Corollary

S1
. Since all of the Horvitz–Thompson estimators are unbiased, it follows that the variance estimators in
Theorem

S2
are unbiased for the upper bounds of the variances. To prove the estimators are conservative, we are going to additionally prove that the estimators are consistent. Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\check{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\left\{\frac{3(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}}+\frac{(4q^{-2}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{\eta_{k}}}\right\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\check{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\Delta{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\tilde{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\left\{\frac{2(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{\eta_{k}+1}}+\frac{(4q^{-1}-2)\mathbf{I}_{N}}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{\eta_{k}}}\right\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\check{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\Delta{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\tilde{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\left\{\frac{\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N}}{2^{-1}q^{2}}+\frac{(2q^{-1}-1)\mathbf{I}_{N}}{2^{-1}q}\right\}\{\Delta{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\tilde{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\},

where
\circ
denotes the Hadamard product. Both
\hat{A}^{d}
and its expectation
A^{d}
scale as
O\{N^{2}(T-p)\}
; the terms with
\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N}
scale as
O\{N^{2}(T-p)\}
and the terms with
\mathbf{I}_{N}
scale as
O\{N(T-p)\}
. To prove that the variance estimators are conservative, we will show that
\{N^{2}(T-p)\}^{-1}\hat{A}^{d}
converges in probability to
\{N^{2}(T-p)\}^{-1}A^{d}
. We consider

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\check{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}^{\top}\left\{\frac{3(\mathbf{J}_{N}-\mathbf{I}_{N})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}}\right\}\{\check{\mathbf{Y}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\circ\check{\mathbf{I}}_{k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\}

in
\hat{A}^{d}
. It can be further rewritten as

\displaystyle 3\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{I}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}}\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1}),

where
\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
is defined in
Theorem

S2
and
\tilde{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
is defined in the first paragraph in
Section

A
.
We further notice that the following term is a Horvitz–Thompson estimator:

\displaystyle\frac{3}{(K-2)N(N-1)}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{I}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}}\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1}).

In our setting, a sample can be expressed with
(ijk)
with
i\neq j
. For example, the outcome is
y_{ijk}=3\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
and the indicator function is
\nu_{ijk}=\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{I}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
. The total population size is then
(K-2)N(N-1)
. The inclusion probability is
\pi_{ijk}=2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}=O(1)
, the average inclusion probability is
\bar{\pi}=\{(K-2)N(N-1)\}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K-2}\sum_{i\neq j}\pi_{ijk}=O(1)
and the normalized version of
\pi_{ijk}
is
\tilde{\pi}_{ijk}=\pi_{ijk}/\bar{\pi}=O(1)
. Letting both
\rho_{y}
and
\rho_{\omega}
in
Lemma

S10
tend to infinity, we have
\kappa_{y}=\max\{|y_{ijk}|\}\leq 3p^{2}B^{2}
and
\kappa_{\omega}=\max\{1/\tilde{\pi}_{ijk}\}=O(1)
. As a result, we can let both
\rho_{y}
and
\rho_{\omega}
tend to infinity. When
|k-k^{\prime}|\geq 3
, the conditional inclusion probability
\pi_{ijk\mid i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}=\pi_{ijk}
because
\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})
and
\check{I}_{j,k^{\prime}}(q\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1})
are independent when
|k-k^{\prime}|\geq 3
. When
|k-k^{\prime}|\leq 2
, we have
\pi_{ijk\mid i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\asymp\pi_{ijk}
, where
a\asymp b
means that
a/b=O(1)
and
b/a=O(1)
, resulting in
|\pi_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}\mid ijk}-\pi_{ijk}|\asymp\pi_{ijk}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle H(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(K-2)^{2}N^{2}(N-1)^{2}}\sum_{(ijk)}\sum_{(i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime})\neq(ijk)}|\tilde{\pi}_{ijk\mid i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}-\tilde{\pi}_{ijk}|

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{(K-2)^{2}N^{2}(N-1)^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{K-1}\sum_{i\neq j}N^{2}|\tilde{\pi}_{ijk}|=O\left(\frac{1}{K-2}\right),

where
H(\cdot)
is defined in
Lemma

S10
.
By
Lemma

S10
, the variance of

\displaystyle\frac{3}{(K-2)N(N-1)}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\check{I}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{I}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}{2^{-\eta_{k}}q^{2\eta_{k}}}\check{Y}_{i,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})\check{Y}_{j,k}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})

is no greater than

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{y}^{2}\kappa_{\omega}}{\bar{\pi}(K-2)N(N-1)}+\kappa_{y}^{2}\kappa_{\omega}H(1)=O\left(\frac{1}{K-2}\right)=O(T^{-1}).

It tends to 0 as
T
approaches infinity. The
L^{2}
convergence implies that this term converges in probability.
Consistency for
\hat{A}^{d}
can be established by considering other terms in
\hat{A}^{d}
, which are all Horvitz–Thompson estimators by similar arguments.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(T-p)N^{2}}(\hat{A}^{d}-A^{d})=o_{P}(1).

The proofs for other quantities are similar. Consequently, the variance estimators are consistent for the upper bounds of the variances, leading us to conclude that they are conservative.

∎

J
Proof of Theorem
3

Definition S1

(
\phi
-dependent random variables,
Hoeffding and Robbins, (1948)
)
.

For any sequence
\{X_{1},X_{2},\ldots\}
, if there exists
\phi
such that for any
t
and
s-r>\phi
, the two sets

\displaystyle(X_{t},X_{t+1},\ldots,X_{t+r}),\quad(X_{t+s},X_{t+s+1},\ldots,X_{t+s+r})

are independent, then the sequence is said to be
\phi
-dependent.

Lemma S11

(Theorem 2.1 of
Romano and Wolf, (2000)
; Lemma 5 of
Han et al., (2024)
)
.

Let
\{X_{n,i}\}
be a triangular array of zero-mean random variables and
\phi\in\mathbb{N}
. For each
n=1,2,\ldots
, let
r=r_{n}
, and suppose that
X_{n,1},X_{n,2},\ldots,X_{n,r}
is a
\phi
-dependent sequence of random variables. Define

\displaystyle C_{n,e_{1},e_{0}}=\mathrm{var}\left(\sum_{i=e_{0}}^{e_{0}+e_{1}-1}X_{n,i}\right),\quad C_{n}=C_{n,r,1}=\mathrm{var}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r}X_{n,i}\right).

For some
\delta>0
and
-1\leq\gamma\leq 1
and
\bar{\phi}>2\phi
, if the following conditions hold,

1.
E|X_{n,i}|^{2+\delta}\leq\Delta_{n}
, for all
i
,

2.
C_{n,e_{1},e_{0}}/e_{1}^{1+\gamma}\leq K_{n}
, for all
e_{0}
and
e_{1}\geq\phi
,

3.
C_{n}/(r\phi^{\gamma})\geq L_{n}
,

4.
\phi/\bar{\phi}\rightarrow 0
,
(K_{n}/L_{n})\cdot(\phi/\bar{\phi})\rightarrow 0
, and
(K_{n}/L_{n})\cdot(\phi/\bar{\phi})^{(1-\gamma)/2}\rightarrow 0
,

5.
\Delta_{n}L_{n}^{-(2+\delta)/2}\bar{\phi}^{\delta/2+(1-\gamma)(2+\delta)/2}r^{-\delta/2}(\phi/\bar{\phi})^{(1-\gamma)(2+\delta)/2}\rightarrow 0
,

then

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}X_{n,i}}{C_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Proof of Theorem
3
.

In the proof, let
n=NT
,
r_{n}=N(T-p)
,
X_{i,t}^{d}(q)=\{N(T-p)\}^{-1/2}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)
and
X_{i,t}^{s}(z)=\{N(T-p)\}^{-1/2}\mathbf{S}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)
, then the following two sequences are both
2b^{*}N
-dependent, for
E\{\mathbf{D}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\mathbf{D}_{i,t+2b^{*}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},q)\}=0
and
E\{\mathbf{S}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\mathbf{S}_{i,t+2b^{*}}(\mathbb{T}^{*},z)\}=0
(we omit
(q)
and
(z)
for notation simplicity):

\displaystyle\{X_{1,p+1}^{d},X_{2,p+1}^{d},\ldots,X_{N,p+1}^{d},X_{1,p+2}^{d},X_{2,p+2}^{d},\ldots,X_{N,p+2}^{d},\ldots,X_{N,T}^{d}\},

\displaystyle\{X_{1,p+1}^{s},X_{2,p+1}^{s},\ldots,X_{N,p+1}^{s},X_{1,p+2}^{s},X_{2,p+2}^{s},\ldots,X_{N,p+2}^{s},\ldots,X_{N,T}^{s}\}.

We then set
\phi=2b^{*}N
in this framework. If we consider multi-center experiments, we can consider the sequence of each center first, and then connect them together. Then the whole sequence is
2b^{*}N_{\max}
-dependent, where
N_{\max}
is the maximum number of units in all centers. Without loss of generality, we can let
\phi=2b^{*}N_{\max}
, as in single-center experiments,
N_{\max}=N
.
Let
C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)=\mathrm{var}\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}X_{i,t}^{d}(q)\}
, where
(i,t)=e
means that
X_{i,t}^{d}
is the
e
-th element in the sequence. Similarly, we can define
C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)=\mathrm{var}\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}X_{i,t}^{s}(z)\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T},q)\right\}\leq\frac{1}{N(T-p)}(e_{1}M_{1}^{d}+2e_{1}\phi M_{2}^{d})=O\left(\frac{e_{1}\phi}{NT}\right),

\displaystyle C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\mathbf{S}_{i,t}(\mathbb{T},z)\right\}\leq\frac{1}{N(T-p)}(e_{1}M_{1}^{s}+2e_{1}\phi M_{2}^{s})=O\left(\frac{e_{1}\phi}{NT}\right),

where
M_{1}^{d}
,
M_{2}^{d}
,
M_{1}^{s}
, and
M_{2}^{s}
are constants associated with the upper bound of variances and covariances.
We further have
C^{d}(q)=C_{N(T-p),1}^{d}(q)=N(T-p)\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)\}=O(\phi)
and
C^{s}(z)=C_{N(T-p),1}^{s}(z)=N(T-p)\mathrm{var}\{\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)\}=O(\phi)
. Next, we will check the five conditions in Lemma
S11
with
\gamma=0
.

(1) There exists
\Delta
such that
E|X_{i,t}^{d}(q)|^{2+\delta}\leq\Delta
and
E|X_{i,t}^{s}(z)|^{2+\delta}\leq\Delta
for all
i
and
t
, because all the potential outcomes are bounded. In the inequality,
\Delta=O\{(NT)^{-1-\delta/2}\}
.

(2) There exist
K^{d}(q)
and
K^{s}(z)
such that
C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)/e_{1}\leq K^{d}(q)
and
C_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)/e_{1}\leq K^{s}(z)
, for all
e_{0}
and
e_{1}\geq\phi
. In the inequality,
K^{d}(q)=O\{\phi/(NT)\}
and
K^{s}(z)=O\{\phi/(NT)\}
.

(3) There exists
L^{d}(q)
and
L^{s}(z)
such that
C^{d}(q)/\{N(T-p)\}\geq L^{d}(q)
and
C^{s}(z)/\{N(T-p)\}\geq L^{s}(z)
. In the inequality,
L^{d}(q)=O\{\phi/(NT)\}
and
L^{s}(z)=O\{\phi/(NT)\}
.

(4) We set
\bar{\phi}=8b^{*}N^{\alpha}T^{\beta}N_{\max}>\phi
. Then the conditions are satisfied when
\phi/\bar{\phi}\rightarrow 0
, which is implied by
N^{-\alpha}T^{-\beta}\rightarrow 0
.

(5) This condition is satisfied when
N^{\alpha-1}T^{\beta-1}N_{\max}\rightarrow 0
.

The conditions required in (4) and (5) are satisfied under Assumption
6
. Therefore, we conclude that the asymptotic normality holds.

∎

K
Proof of Theorem
4

In this section, we set
m
as the order of the carryover effects, and
p
is the experimenter’s knowledge of
m
. When
p\geq m
, the estimation and inference methods will still hold, for
Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})=Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{m+1})
. However, when
p<m
, the estimator will no longer be unbiased and the effects are not well-defined. Let
\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t_{1}:t_{2}}
denote the observed treated probability and
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t_{1}:t_{2}}
denote the observed treatment path for unit
i
during the time period
[t_{1},t_{2}]
. The
m
-misspecified lag-
p
direct effect and spillover effect can be defined as

\displaystyle\tau^{d}_{[m]}(q)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{T-p}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sum_{t=p+1}^{m}\left\{Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\mathbf{1}_{p+1})-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\mathbf{0}_{p+1})\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=m+1}^{T}\Big\{Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\mathbf{1}_{p+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\mathbf{0}_{p+1})\Big\}\bigg],

\displaystyle\tau^{s}_{[m]}(z)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{T-p}

\displaystyle E\bigg[\sum_{t=p+1}^{m}\left\{Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},z\mathbf{1}_{p+1})-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,1:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},z\mathbf{1}_{p+1})\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=m+1}^{T}\Big\{Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},z\mathbf{1}_{p+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:t-p-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},z\mathbf{1}_{p+1})\Big\}\bigg].

Lemma S12
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
,
r_{q_{1}}=r_{q_{2}}=0.5
, and the minimax optimal design
\mathbb{T}^{*}
, for
q=q_{1},q_{2}
and
z=0,1
, we have (1) for
p<m
,
t\geq m+1
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)\leq t-m
,

\displaystyle E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}\right\}

\displaystyle=Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{m+1})-Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{m+1}),

\displaystyle E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}\right\}

\displaystyle=Y_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{m+1})-Y_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{m+1});

(2) for
p<m
,
t\geq m+1
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)>t-m
,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\bigg[\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}\right\}\mid\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}})

\displaystyle-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{0}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}}),

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\bigg[\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}\right\}\mid\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}})

\displaystyle-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q_{2}\mathbf{1}^{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}});

(3) for
p<m
,
p+1\leq t\leq m
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)=1
,

\displaystyle E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}\right\}

\displaystyle=Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{t+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{t+1})-Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{t+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{t+1}),

\displaystyle E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}\right\}

\displaystyle=Y_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{t+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{t+1})-Y_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{t+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{t+1});

(4) for
p<m
,
p+1\leq t\leq m
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)>1
,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\bigg[\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{}\mathbf{0}_{p+1})}\right\}\mid\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}})

\displaystyle\quad-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{0}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}}),

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\bigg[\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q_{2}\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}\right\}\mid\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q_{1}\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}})

\displaystyle\quad-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q_{2}\mathbf{1}^{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,1:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}}).

Proof of Lemma
S12
.

When
t\geq m+1
, the inequality
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)\leq t-m
indicates that the set of decision points of time
t
remains unchanged between
p
and
m
, i.e,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}^{p}(t)=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}^{m}(t)
.
With probability
\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})\neq 0
,
I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})=1
, and then
Y_{i,t}=Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{m+1})
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}\right\}=Y_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{m+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{m+1}).

When
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)>t-m
,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}^{m}(t)
differs from
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}^{p}(t)
. With conditional probability

\mathrm{pr}\{\mathbf{Q}_{t-p:t}=q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-p:t}=z\mathbf{1}_{p+1}\mid\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}\}\neq 0
and conditional on
\mathbf{Q}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}
,

Y_{i,t}=Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}}).

Then we obtain

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\bigg[Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}(q\mathbf{1}_{p+1},{z}\mathbf{1}_{p+1})}-Y_{i,t}(\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},q\mathbf{1}_{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},z\mathbf{1}^{t-\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}(t-p)+1}})\mid

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1},\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{obs}}_{i,t-m:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}^{*}}(t-p)-1}\bigg]=0.

The results for
p+1\leq t\leq m
can be proved similarly.
∎

By
Lemma

S12
, the expectations of the Horvitz–Thompson estimators are
\tau^{d}_{[m]}(q)
and
\tau^{s}_{[m]}(z)
. Without additional information, these estimators exhibit a non-negligible bias when compared to
\tau^{d}(q)
and
\tau^{s}(z)
. The expressions for the variances are more complex, making it challenging to derive conservative variance estimators from the observed data. However, despite the bias present in the estimators, they remain asymptotically normal, as demonstrated in
Theorem

4
. Next, we will prove
Theorem

4
.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

We only consider
p<m
, as the proof for the case of
p>m
is similar. In this case, with the same trick as in the proof of
Theorem

3
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)-E\{\hat{\tau}^{d}(q)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}^{(m)}(q),

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)-E\{\hat{\tau}^{s}(z)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{S}_{i,t}^{(m)}(z),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,t}^{(m)}(q)

\displaystyle=\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})}\right\}-E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q}\mathbf{1},{}\mathbf{0})}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{i,t}^{(m)}(q)

\displaystyle=\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}\right\}-E\left\{Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q_{1}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}-Y_{i,t}\frac{I_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}{\mathrm{pr}_{i,t}({q_{2}}\mathbf{1},{z}\mathbf{1})}\right\}.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
3
, let
r_{n}=N(T-p)
,
\tilde{X}^{d}_{i,t}(q)=\{N(T-p)\}^{-1/2}\mathbf{D}_{i,t}^{(m)}(q)
and
\tilde{X}^{s}_{i,t}(z)=\{N(T-p)\}^{-1/2}\mathbf{S}_{i,t}^{(m)}(z)
, then,
\{\tilde{X}_{1,1}^{d},\tilde{X}_{1,2}^{d},\ldots\}
and
\{\tilde{X}_{1,1}^{s},\tilde{X}_{1,2}^{s},\ldots\}
are sequences of
\tilde{\phi}
-independent random variables, respectively, where
\tilde{\phi}=(\lceil m/p\rceil+1)b^{*}N_{\max}
. Let
\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)=\mathrm{var}\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\tilde{X}_{i,t}^{d}(q)\}
and
\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)=\mathrm{var}\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\tilde{X}_{n,i}^{s}(z)\}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\tilde{X}_{i,t}^{d}(q)\right\}\leq\frac{1}{N(T-p)}(e_{1}\tilde{M}_{1}^{d}+2e_{1}\tilde{\phi}\tilde{M}_{2}^{d})=O\left(\frac{e_{1}\tilde{\phi}}{NT}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(T-p)}\mathrm{var}\left\{\sum_{(i,t)=e_{0}}^{(i,t)=e_{0}+e_{1}-1}\tilde{X}_{i,t}^{s}(z)\right\}\leq\frac{1}{N(T-p)}(e_{1}\tilde{M}_{1}^{s}+2e_{1}\tilde{\phi}\tilde{M}_{2}^{s})=O\left(\frac{e_{1}\tilde{\phi}}{NT}\right),

where
\tilde{M}_{1}^{d}
,
\tilde{M}_{2}^{d}
,
\tilde{M}_{1}^{s}
and
\tilde{M}_{2}^{s}
are constants associated with the upper bound of variances and covariances. We further have
\tilde{C}^{d}(q)=\tilde{C}_{N(T-p),1}^{d}(q)=O(\tilde{\phi})
and
\tilde{C}^{s}(z)=\tilde{C}_{N(T-p),1}^{s}(z)=O(\tilde{\phi})
.
We will check the five conditions in Lemma
S11
with
\gamma=0
.

(1) There exists
\tilde{\Delta}
such that
E|\tilde{X}_{i,t}^{d}(q)|^{2+\delta}\leq\tilde{\Delta}
and
E|\tilde{X}_{i,t}^{s}(z)|^{2+\delta}\leq\tilde{\Delta}
for all
i
and
t
, because all the potential outcomes are bounded. In the inequality,
\tilde{\Delta}=O\{(NT)^{-1-\delta/2}\}
.

(2) There exist
\tilde{K}^{d}(q)
and
\tilde{K}^{s}(z)
such that
\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{d}(q)/e_{1}\leq\tilde{K}^{d}(q)
and
\tilde{C}_{e_{1},e_{0}}^{s}(z)/e_{1}\leq\tilde{K}^{s}(z)
, for all
e_{0}
and
e_{1}\geq\tilde{\phi}
. In the inequality,
\tilde{K}^{d}(q)=O\{\tilde{\phi}/(NT)\}
and
\tilde{K}^{s}(z)=O\{\tilde{\phi}/(NT)\}
.

(3) There exist
\tilde{L}^{d}(q)
and
\tilde{L}^{s}(z)
such that
\tilde{C}^{d}(q)/\{N(T-p)\}\geq\tilde{L}^{d}(q)
and
\tilde{C}^{s}(z)/\{N(T-p)\}\geq\tilde{L}^{s}(z)
. In the inequality,
\tilde{L}^{d}(q)=O\{\tilde{\phi}/(NT)\}
and
\tilde{L}^{s}(z)=O\{\tilde{\phi}/(NT)\}
.

(4) We set
\tilde{\bar{\phi}}=4(\lceil m/p\rceil+1)b^{*}N^{\alpha}T^{\beta}N_{\max}>\tilde{\phi}
. Then, this condition is satisfied when
\tilde{\phi}/\tilde{\bar{\phi}}\rightarrow 0
, which is implied by
N^{-\alpha}T^{-\beta}\rightarrow 0
.

(5) This condition is satisfied when
N^{\alpha-1}T^{\beta-1}N_{\max}\rightarrow 0
.

Therefore, the conclusion holds.
∎

L

S2
, and

1
. The conclusions drawn are consistent with those under
\mathbb{T}^{*}_{1}

S3
